## Ratio bound between higher-kkterms and the leading slot.

We first boundF~λ,kPois,ro​(w)\tilde{F}_{\lambda,k}^{\mathrm{Pois,ro}}(w)from (S2.2.1), which splits into two pieces:(λ−1)​λk−1​[4​Mk​(w)​𝟏​{k​even}+3​Mk−1​(w)​Mk+1​(w)​𝟏​{k​odd}]⏟Term A\displaystyle\underbrace{(\lambda-1)\lambda^{k-1}[4M_{k}(w)\mathbf{1}\{k\text{ even}\}+3\sqrt{M_{k-1}(w)M_{k+1}(w)}\mathbf{1}\{k\text{ odd}\}]}_{\text{Term A}}+(λ−1)​λk−1​B~k​(w)​Λk​(λ)⏟Term B.\displaystyle+\underbrace{(\lambda-1)\lambda^{k-1}\tilde{B}_{k}(w)\Lambda_{k}(\lambda)}_{\text{Term B}}.

Term A is bounded directly by the uniform envelope (S41): on the finite range2≤k≤m−12\leq k\leq m-1,B~k​(w)≤Cm,B​(w​C/σ)k\tilde{B}_{k}(w)\leq C_{m,B}(wC/\sigma)^{k}covers bothMkM_{k}for evenkkandMk−1​Mk+1\sqrt{M_{k-1}M_{k+1}}for oddkk, so Term A=O​{λk​(w​C/σ)k}=O\{\lambda^{k}(wC/\sigma)^{k}\}uniformly over this finite range.

For Term B we must control theΛk​(λ)\Lambda_{k}(\lambda)factor of (S2.2.1), written asΛk​(λ)=∑j=0k(kj)​λλ−1​[Pj​(λ)−1]\Lambda_{k}(\lambda)=\sum_{j=0}^{k}\binom{k}{j}\frac{\lambda}{\lambda-1}[P_{j}(\lambda)-1]withPj​(λ):=∏ℓ=0j−1(1−ℓ/λ)​∏ℓ=0k−j−1(1+(ℓ−1)/λ)P_{j}(\lambda):=\prod_{\ell=0}^{j-1}(1-\ell/\lambda)\prod_{\ell=0}^{k-j-1}(1+(\ell-1)/\lambda).

We provide a uniform bound of this under the fixed-truncation regime. The functionF~λ,kPois,ro\tilde{F}_{\lambda,k}^{\mathrm{Pois,ro}}is defined for2≤k≤m−12\leq k\leq m-1, while onlyk=3,…,m−1k=3,\ldots,m-1appears in the Taylor sum because thek=2k=2term is the leadingq2​Φλq^{2}\Phi_{\lambda}term. For every integerλ≥2\lambda\geq 2and0≤ℓ≤k−10\leq\ell\leq k-1, with2≤k≤m−12\leq k\leq m-1, the two product factors are bounded by constants depending only onmm:|1−ℓ/λ|≤1+(m−2)/2|1-\ell/\lambda|\leq 1+(m-2)/2and|1+(ℓ−1)/λ|≤1+(m−2)/2|1+(\ell-1)/\lambda|\leq 1+(m-2)/2. Hence|Pj​(λ)|≤Cm,Λ|P_{j}(\lambda)|\leq C_{m,\Lambda}and|Pj​(λ)−1|≤Cm,Λ|P_{j}(\lambda)-1|\leq C_{m,\Lambda}uniformly overjj,kk, andλ\lambda. With(kj)\binom{k}{j}summing to2k≤2m2^{k}\leq 2^{m}andλ/(λ−1)≤2\lambda/(\lambda-1)\leq 2,|Λk​(λ)|≤Cm,Λuniformly for integer​λ≥2​and​2≤k≤m−1,\displaystyle|\Lambda_{k}(\lambda)|\leq C_{m,\Lambda}\qquad\text{uniformly for integer }\lambda\geq 2\text{ and }2\leq k\leq m-1,

which is a finite constant at fixed truncation order.

Combining the two terms gives the pointwise uniform bound|F~λ,kPois,ro​(w)|≤Cm,F​λk​(w​Cσ)k,0≤w≤1/nS,3≤k≤m−1,\displaystyle|\tilde{F}_{\lambda,k}^{\mathrm{Pois,ro}}(w)|\leq C_{m,F}\lambda^{k}\Bigl(\frac{wC}{\sigma}\Bigr)^{k},\qquad 0\leq w\leq 1/n_{S},\quad 3\leq k\leq m-1,

for a constantCm,FC_{m,F}depending only on the fixed truncation order. Therefore the expectation is bounded by integrating this pointwise inequality:𝔼w¯​[F~λ,kPois,ro​(w¯)]\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{\bar{w}}[\tilde{F}_{\lambda,k}^{\mathrm{Pois,ro}}(\bar{w})]≤Cm,F​λk​(C/σ)k​𝔼​[w¯k]\displaystyle\leq C_{m,F}\lambda^{k}(C/\sigma)^{k}{\mathbb{E}}[\bar{w}^{k}]≤Cm,F​λk​(C/σ)2​𝔼​[w¯2]​(C/(σ​nS))k−2,k≥3.\displaystyle\leq C_{m,F}\lambda^{k}(C/\sigma)^{2}{\mathbb{E}}[\bar{w}^{2}](C/(\sigma n_{S}))^{k-2},\qquad k\geq 3.

Comparing to the leading slotq2​𝔼​[Φλ​(w¯)]q^{2}{\mathbb{E}}[\Phi_{\lambda}(\bar{w})]from (S38),qk​𝔼​[F~λ,kPois,ro​(w¯)]/k!q2​𝔼​[Φλ​(w¯)]=O​(1k!​(q​λ⋅C/(σ​nS))k−2),k≥3.\displaystyle\frac{q^{k}{\mathbb{E}}[\tilde{F}_{\lambda,k}^{\mathrm{Pois,ro}}(\bar{w})]/k!}{q^{2}{\mathbb{E}}[\Phi_{\lambda}(\bar{w})]}=O(\tfrac{1}{k!}(q\lambda\cdot C/(\sigma n_{S}))^{k-2}),\qquad k\geq 3.(S42)

The suppression in (S42) is controlled, at the current Rényi order, by the single small parameterϱ=ϱ​(λ;σ,nS):=q​λ⋅C/(σ​nS)\varrho=\varrho(\lambda;\sigma,n_{S}):=q\lambda\cdot C/(\sigma n_{S}); below we write simplyϱ\varrhowhileλ\lambdais fixed. Each Taylor term has the explicit formϱk−2/k!\varrho^{k-2}/k!. Closing the Taylor sum,∑k=3m−1qk​𝔼​[F~λ,kPois,ro​(w¯)]/k!q2​𝔼​[Φλ​(w¯)]≤Cm,F​∑k=3m−1ϱk−2k!≤Cm,F​∑k=3∞ϱk−2k!=Cm,F​eϱ−1−ϱ−12​ϱ2ϱ2,\displaystyle\sum_{k=3}^{m-1}\frac{q^{k}{\mathbb{E}}[\tilde{F}_{\lambda,k}^{\mathrm{Pois,ro}}(\bar{w})]/k!}{q^{2}{\mathbb{E}}[\Phi_{\lambda}(\bar{w})]}\leq C_{m,F}\sum_{k=3}^{m-1}\frac{\varrho^{k-2}}{k!}\leq C_{m,F}\sum_{k=3}^{\infty}\frac{\varrho^{k-2}}{k!}=C_{m,F}\frac{e^{\varrho}-1-\varrho-\tfrac{1}{2}\varrho^{2}}{\varrho^{2}},(S43)

whereCm,F<∞C_{m,F}<\inftydepends only on the fixed truncation ordermm. The closed form follows by writing∑k≥3ϱk/k!=eϱ−1−ϱ−12​ϱ2\sum_{k\geq 3}\varrho^{k}/k!=e^{\varrho}-1-\varrho-\tfrac{1}{2}\varrho^{2}and dividing byϱ2\varrho^{2}. Asϱ→0\varrho\to 0, the Taylor expansioneϱ−1−ϱ−12​ϱ2=16​ϱ3+O​(ϱ4)e^{\varrho}-1-\varrho-\tfrac{1}{2}\varrho^{2}=\tfrac{1}{6}\varrho^{3}+O(\varrho^{4})givesCm,F​∑k=3∞ϱk−2k!=Cm,F​{ϱ6+O​(ϱ2)}=O​(ϱ)C_{m,F}\sum_{k=3}^{\infty}\frac{\varrho^{k-2}}{k!}=C_{m,F}\{\frac{\varrho}{6}+O(\varrho^{2})\}=O(\varrho)asϱ→0\varrho\to 0, dominated by thek=3k=3slot.

## Separate bound on the integral remainderE~λ,mPois,ro\tilde{E}_{\lambda,m}^{\mathrm{Pois,ro}}.

The Lagrange-type remainder of (S25)-(S26) is structurally distinct from the Taylor terms and admits its own bound. WritingAj:=∏ℓ=0j−1|λ−ℓ|A_{j}:=\prod_{\ell=0}^{j-1}|\lambda-\ell|andBm−j:=∏ℓ=0m−j−1(λ+ℓ−1)B_{m-j}:=\prod_{\ell=0}^{m-j-1}(\lambda+\ell-1)for the twoλ\lambda-factor products in (S25), we bound each piece in turn.

## Effective range ofjj.

The factorAjA_{j}vanishes atℓ=λ\ell=\lambda, soj≥λ+1j\geq\lambda+1contributes zero, and(mj)=0\binom{m}{j}=0forj>mj>m. Hence the sum runs effectively overj∈{0,1,…,min⁡(λ,m)}j\in\{0,1,\ldots,\min(\lambda,m)\}.

## Per-termλ\lambda-factor bounds.

Aj=λ​(λ−1)​⋯​(λ−j+1)≤λjA_{j}=\lambda(\lambda-1)\cdots(\lambda-j+1)\leq\lambda^{j}andBm−j=(λ−1)​λ​⋯​(λ+m−j−2)≤(λ+m)m−jB_{m-j}=(\lambda-1)\lambda\cdots(\lambda+m-j-2)\leq(\lambda+m)^{m-j}, each by bounding every factor by its largest member.

## Binomial-theorem summation overjj.

Summing the per-term
bound above weighted by(mj)\binom{m}{j},∑j=0m(mj)​Aj​Bm−j≤∑j=0m(mj)​λj​(λ+m)m−j=(λ+λ+m)m=(2​λ+m)m,\displaystyle\sum_{j=0}^{m}\binom{m}{j}A_{j}B_{m-j}\leq\sum_{j=0}^{m}\binom{m}{j}\lambda^{j}(\lambda+m)^{m-j}=(\lambda+\lambda+m)^{m}=(2\lambda+m)^{m},

by the binomial theorem applied to(a+b)m(a+b)^{m}witha=λa=\lambdaandb=λ+mb=\lambda+m. Forλ≫m\lambda\gg m,(2​λ+m)m=(2​λ)m​(1+m/(2​λ))m≤(2​λ)m​em2/(2​λ)=O​(λm)(2\lambda+m)^{m}=(2\lambda)^{m}(1+m/(2\lambda))^{m}\leq(2\lambda)^{m}\,e^{m^{2}/(2\lambda)}=O(\lambda^{m}).

## The(1−q)(1-q)factor.

This is the only place where the
sampling-ratio condition is used. Uniformly injj,(1−q)−(λ+m−j−1)≤(1−q)−(λ+m−1)(1-q)^{-(\lambda+m-j-1)}\leq(1-q)^{-(\lambda+m-1)}and, more precisely, the integral remainder carries the multiplicative factor(1−q)−(λ+m−1)≤exp⁡{(λ+m−1)​q1−q}.\displaystyle(1-q)^{-(\lambda+m-1)}\leq\exp\left\{\frac{(\lambda+m-1)q}{1-q}\right\}.

At the optimizing order used below, and at the leading boundary scale,λ⋆≍L/ϵ\lambda^{\star}\asymp L/\epsilonwithL=log⁡(1/δ)L=\log(1/\delta). Therefore this factor is uniformly absorbed into the constants whenq​L/{(1−q)​ϵ}=O​(1)qL/\{(1-q)\epsilon\}=O(1). Ifqqis bounded away from one, this reduces to the simpler conditionq​L/ϵ=O​(1)qL/\epsilon=O(1). Without such a condition the same proof would carry an additionalexp⁡{q​L/((1−q)​ϵ)}\exp\{qL/((1-q)\epsilon)\}multiplier in the integral-remainder term, and the clean leading expansion would no longer follow from the stated small-o conditions alone.

## TheΘ\Thetafactor.

Forj<λj<\lambda, (S26) readsΘλ,m,j​(q,w)=B~m​(w)+∑ℓ=0λ−jqℓ​cℓ​B~m+ℓ​(w)\Theta_{\lambda,m,j}(q,w)=\tilde{B}_{m}(w)+\sum_{\ell=0}^{\lambda-j}q^{\ell}c_{\ell}\tilde{B}_{m+\ell}(w)withcℓ:=(λ−j)!​m!(λ−j−ℓ)!​(m+ℓ)!c_{\ell}:=\frac{(\lambda-j)!\,m!}{(\lambda-j-\ell)!(m+\ell)!}. Each factor in the numerator ofcℓc_{\ell}is at mostλ\lambda, each in the denominator is at leastm+1m+1, socℓ=(λ−j)​(λ−j−1)​⋯​(λ−j−ℓ+1)(m+1)​(m+2)​⋯​(m+ℓ)≤(λm+1)ℓ.\displaystyle c_{\ell}=\frac{(\lambda-j)(\lambda-j-1)\cdots(\lambda-j-\ell+1)}{(m+1)(m+2)\cdots(m+\ell)}\leq\Bigl(\frac{\lambda}{m+1}\Bigr)^{\ell}.

Combining withB~m+ℓ​(w)=O​((w​C/σ)m+ℓ)=O​((w​C/σ)m)⋅(C/(σ​nS))ℓ\tilde{B}_{m+\ell}(w)=O((wC/\sigma)^{m+\ell})=O((wC/\sigma)^{m})\cdot(C/(\sigma n_{S}))^{\ell}(via (S41) and the a.s. boundw≤1/nSw\leq 1/n_{S}),|Θλ,m,j​(q,w)|≤B~m​(w)​[1+O​(1)​∑ℓ=0∞(q​λ​C(m+1)​σ​nS)ℓ]=O​(B~m​(w)),\displaystyle|\Theta_{\lambda,m,j}(q,w)|\leq\tilde{B}_{m}(w)\Bigl[1+O(1)\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}(\tfrac{q\lambda C}{(m+1)\sigma n_{S}})^{\ell}\Bigr]=O(\tilde{B}_{m}(w)),

where the geometric series converges wheneverϱ=q​λ​C/(σ​nS)<m+1\varrho=q\lambda C/(\sigma n_{S})<m+1, which is precisely the same small-ϱ\varrhocondition used in (S43). Forj=λj=\lambda,Θλ,m,λ=B~m​(w)\Theta_{\lambda,m,\lambda}=\tilde{B}_{m}(w)directly. So|Θλ,m,j​(q,w)|=O​(B~m​(w))|\Theta_{\lambda,m,j}(q,w)|=O(\tilde{B}_{m}(w))uniformly injj.

## Assembling.

Putting the bounds above together,|E~λ,mPois,ro​(q,w)|≤qmm!⋅O​(B~m​(w))⋅O​(1)⋅(2​λ+m)m=O​((q​λ)mm!​B~m​(w))=O​((q​λ)mm!​(w​C/σ)m),\displaystyle\begin{aligned} |\tilde{E}_{\lambda,m}^{\mathrm{Pois,ro}}(q,w)|&\leq\frac{q^{m}}{m!}\cdot O(\tilde{B}_{m}(w))\cdot O(1)\cdot(2\lambda+m)^{m}\\
&=O(\tfrac{(q\lambda)^{m}}{m!}\tilde{B}_{m}(w))=O(\tfrac{(q\lambda)^{m}}{m!}(wC/\sigma)^{m}),\end{aligned}

where the second equality absorbs(2​λ+m)m/λm=O​(1)(2\lambda+m)^{m}/\lambda^{m}=O(1)from the binomial summation and the last usesB~m​(w)=O​{(w​C/σ)m}\tilde{B}_{m}(w)=O\{(wC/\sigma)^{m}\}from (S41). Taking expectation againstw¯\bar{w}and applying the a.s. boundw¯≤1/nS\bar{w}\leq 1/n_{S}via𝔼​[w¯m]=𝔼​[w¯2⋅w¯m−2]≤𝔼​[w¯2]/nSm−2{\mathbb{E}}[\bar{w}^{m}]={\mathbb{E}}[\bar{w}^{2}\cdot\bar{w}^{m-2}]\leq{\mathbb{E}}[\bar{w}^{2}]/n_{S}^{m-2},𝔼​[E~λ,mPois,ro​(q,w¯)]≤O​(qm​λmm!​(C/σ)m​𝔼​[w¯2]/nSm−2)=O​(qm​λmm!​(C/σ)2​𝔼​[w¯2]​(C/(σ​nS))m−2).\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\tilde{E}_{\lambda,m}^{\mathrm{Pois,ro}}(q,\bar{w})]\leq O(\tfrac{q^{m}\lambda^{m}}{m!}(C/\sigma)^{m}{\mathbb{E}}[\bar{w}^{2}]/n_{S}^{m-2})=O(\tfrac{q^{m}\lambda^{m}}{m!}(C/\sigma)^{2}{\mathbb{E}}[\bar{w}^{2}](C/(\sigma n_{S}))^{m-2}).

Dividing byq2​𝔼​[Φλ​(w¯)]q^{2}{\mathbb{E}}[\Phi_{\lambda}(\bar{w})]from (S38),𝔼​[E~λ,mPois,ro​(q,w¯)]q2​𝔼​[Φλ​(w¯)]=O​(1m!​(q​λ⋅C/(σ​nS))m−2)=O​(ϱm−2/m!),m≥3.\displaystyle\frac{{\mathbb{E}}[\tilde{E}_{\lambda,m}^{\mathrm{Pois,ro}}(q,\bar{w})]}{q^{2}{\mathbb{E}}[\Phi_{\lambda}(\bar{w})]}=O(\tfrac{1}{m!}(q\lambda\cdot C/(\sigma n_{S}))^{m-2})=O(\varrho^{m-2}/m!),\qquad m\geq 3.(S44)

## Summary: characterization ofexp⁡{Aℳt​(λ)}\exp\{A_{\mathcal{M}_{t}}(\lambda)\}.

Combining the per-iteration bound (S33) from Step 2 with the linearization (S38) of the leading slot, the bound (S42)-(S43) on the higher-kkTaylor terms, and the bound (S44) on the integral remainder, the per-iteration moment-generating function admits the explicit characterizationexp⁡{Aℳt​(λ)}\displaystyle\exp\{A_{\mathcal{M}_{t}}(\lambda)\}≤1+q2​𝔼w¯​[Φλ​(w¯)]+∑k=3m−1qkk!​𝔼w¯​[F~λ,kPois,ro​(w¯)]+𝔼w¯​[E~λ,mPois,ro​(q,w¯)]\displaystyle\leq 1+q^{2}{\mathbb{E}}_{\bar{w}}[\Phi_{\lambda}(\bar{w})]+\sum_{k=3}^{m-1}\tfrac{q^{k}}{k!}{\mathbb{E}}_{\bar{w}}[\tilde{F}_{\lambda,k}^{\mathrm{Pois,ro}}(\bar{w})]+{\mathbb{E}}_{\bar{w}}[\tilde{E}_{\lambda,m}^{\mathrm{Pois,ro}}(q,\bar{w})]≤1+q2​𝔼w¯​[Φλ​(w¯)]​(1+O​(ϱ))\displaystyle\leq 1+q^{2}{\mathbb{E}}_{\bar{w}}[\Phi_{\lambda}(\bar{w})](1+O(\varrho))=1+2​q2​λ​(λ−1)​γ​(γ+1)​C2σ2​(S​γ+α)​(S​γ+α+1)​nS2​(1+RΦ​(σ,nS;C)+O​(ϱ)),\displaystyle=1+\frac{2q^{2}\lambda(\lambda-1)\gamma(\gamma+1)C^{2}}{\sigma^{2}(S\gamma+\alpha)(S\gamma+\alpha+1)n_{S}^{2}}(1+R_{\Phi}(\sigma,n_{S};C)+O(\varrho)),

whereϱ\varrhodenotes the same current-order quantityq​λ​C/(σ​nS)q\lambda C/(\sigma n_{S}). Taking logarithms and usinglog⁡(1+x)=x+O​(x2)\log(1+x)=x+O(x^{2}),Aℳt​(λ)≤2​q2​λ​(λ−1)​γ​(γ+1)​C2σ2​(S​γ+α)​(S​γ+α+1)​nS2​(1+O​(RΦ​(σ,nS;C)+ϱ)).\displaystyle A_{\mathcal{M}_{t}}(\lambda)\leq\frac{2q^{2}\lambda(\lambda-1)\gamma(\gamma+1)C^{2}}{\sigma^{2}(S\gamma+\alpha)(S\gamma+\alpha+1)n_{S}^{2}}(1+O(R_{\Phi}(\sigma,n_{S};C)+\varrho)).

This is the explicit per-iteration rate ready for composition over theTTouter iterations andKKinner Monte Carlo replicates. Along sequences with fixed truncation order,C/(σ​nS)→0C/(\sigma n_{S})\to 0, andϱ​(λ;σ,nS)=o​(1)\varrho(\lambda;\sigma,n_{S})=o(1)at the Rényi order under consideration, the resulting expansion givesγMtot​(λ;σ):=Aℳ​(λ)λ−1≤c​(σ)​λ​(1+O​(RΦ​(σ,nS;C)+ϱ)),c=c​(σ):=2​T​K​q2​γ​(γ+1)​C2σ2​(S​γ+α)​(S​γ+α+1)​nS2.\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \gamma_{M}^{\mathrm{tot}}(\lambda;\sigma)&:=\frac{A_{\mathcal{M}}(\lambda)}{\lambda-1}\leq c(\sigma)\lambda(1+O(R_{\Phi}(\sigma,n_{S};C)+\varrho)),\\
c&=c(\sigma):=\frac{2TKq^{2}\gamma(\gamma+1)C^{2}}{\sigma^{2}(S\gamma+\alpha)(S\gamma+\alpha+1)n_{S}^{2}}.\end{aligned}(S45)

## Conversion to approximate DP.

Step 3 leavesℳ\mathcal{M}certified as(λ,γMtot​(λ;σ))(\lambda,\gamma_{M}^{\mathrm{tot}}(\lambda;\sigma))-RDP at every integerλ≥2\lambda\geq 2, with the slope-rate decompositionγMtot​(λ;σ)≤c​(σ)​λ​(1+Δλ),Δλ=Crem​{RΦ​(σ,nS;C)+ϱ},\displaystyle\gamma_{M}^{\mathrm{tot}}(\lambda;\sigma)\leq c(\sigma)\lambda(1+\Delta_{\lambda}),\qquad\Delta_{\lambda}=C_{\mathrm{rem}}\{R_{\Phi}(\sigma,n_{S};C)+\varrho\},

for some constantCremC_{\text{rem}}, wherec​(σ)c(\sigma)isc​(σ):=2​T​K​q2​γ​(γ+1)​C2σ2​(S​γ+α)​(S​γ+α+1)​nS2,\displaystyle c(\sigma):=\frac{2TKq^{2}\gamma(\gamma+1)C^{2}}{\sigma^{2}(S\gamma+\alpha)(S\gamma+\alpha+1)n_{S}^{2}},(S46)

andΔλ\Delta_{\lambda}is the relative slack inherited from (S45). Hereϱ\varrhomeansϱ​(λ;σ,nS)=q​λ​C/(σ​nS)\varrho(\lambda;\sigma,n_{S})=q\lambda C/(\sigma n_{S})at the same integer orderλ\lambda. Mironov’s RDP-to-DP conversion(Mironov2017, Prop. 3)bounds the privacy budget at every integerλ≥2\lambda\geq 2byϵ​(λ,δ)≤c​(σ)​λ​(1+Δλ)+log⁡(1/δ)λ−1.\displaystyle\epsilon(\lambda,\delta)\leq c(\sigma)\lambda(1+\Delta_{\lambda})+\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{\lambda-1}.(S47)

To extract the leading scale, writeL:=log⁡(1/δ)L:=\log(1/\delta)and dropΔλ\Delta_{\lambda}from the optimization. The reduced objectiveψ​(λ):=c​(σ)​λ+L/(λ−1)\psi(\lambda):=c(\sigma)\lambda+L/(\lambda-1)has continuous minimizerλ⋆=1+L/c​(σ),ψ​(λ⋆)=c​(σ)+2​c​(σ)​L,\displaystyle\lambda^{\star}=1+\sqrt{L/c(\sigma)},\qquad\psi(\lambda^{\star})=c(\sigma)+2\sqrt{c(\sigma)L},

where the continuous optimizer is used only to read off the leading scale. If Birrell’s integer-order restriction is enforced literally, takeλ^⋆=max⁡{2,⌈λ⋆⌉}\hat{\lambda}^{\star}=\max\{2,\lceil\lambda^{\star}\rceil\}. Writings=λ⋆−1=L/c​(σ)s=\lambda^{\star}-1=\sqrt{L/c(\sigma)}andd=λ^⋆−λ⋆∈[0,1)d=\hat{\lambda}^{\star}-\lambda^{\star}\in[0,1)givesψ​(λ^⋆)−ψ​(λ⋆)=c​(σ)​d2/(s+d)≤c​(σ)\psi(\hat{\lambda}^{\star})-\psi(\lambda^{\star})=c(\sigma)d^{2}/(s+d)\leq c(\sigma), and at the calibrated scale, wherec​(σ)≍ϵ2/Lc(\sigma)\asymp\epsilon^{2}/L, this isO​(ϵ2/L)=ϵ​O​(ϵ/L)O(\epsilon^{2}/L)=\epsilon\,O(\epsilon/L). Hence, the integer rounding does not change the leading calibration. The regime conditionc​(σ)≤Lc(\sigma)\leq Lis enough to keep the linear term no larger than the square-root term:εlead​(δ)=c​(σ)+2​c​(σ)​L≲c​(σ)​L=C​q​T​K​L​γ​(γ+1)/[(S​γ+α)​(S​γ+α+1)]σ​nS.\displaystyle\varepsilon_{\mathrm{lead}}(\delta)=c(\sigma)+2\sqrt{c(\sigma)L}\lesssim\sqrt{c(\sigma)L}=\frac{Cq\sqrt{TKL\gamma(\gamma+1)/[(S\gamma+\alpha)(S\gamma+\alpha+1)]}}{\sigma n_{S}}.(S48)

Requiringεlead≲ϵ\varepsilon_{\mathrm{lead}}\lesssim\epsilonforces the noise scale to obeyσ≳C​T​K​q2​L​γ​(γ+1)nS​(S​γ+α)​(S​γ+α+1)​ϵ,\displaystyle\sigma\gtrsim\frac{C\sqrt{TKq^{2}L\gamma(\gamma+1)}}{n_{S}\sqrt{(S\gamma+\alpha)(S\gamma+\alpha+1)}\epsilon},(S49)

which is the leading-order scale of Theorem2.1.

## The dropped slack.

It remains to verify that droppingΔλ\Delta_{\lambda}in the optimization costs nothing at leading order. Substitutingλ⋆−1=L/c​(σ)\lambda^{\star}-1=\sqrt{L/c(\sigma)}intoϱ​(λ;σ,nS)=q​λ​C/(σ​nS)\varrho(\lambda;\sigma,n_{S})=q\lambda C/(\sigma n_{S})and then evaluating atλ=λ⋆\lambda=\lambda^{\star}splits the relative slack into aσ\sigma-dependent piece and a purely external piece:ϱ|λ=λ⋆=q​Cσ​nS+L/c​(σ)⋅q​Cσ​nS=q​Cσ​nS+ϱ⋆,\displaystyle\left.\varrho\right|_{\lambda=\lambda^{\star}}=\frac{qC}{\sigma n_{S}}+\sqrt{L/c(\sigma)}\cdot\frac{qC}{\sigma n_{S}}=\frac{qC}{\sigma n_{S}}+\varrho^{\star},

where theσ​nS/C\sigma n_{S}/Cimplicit inL/c​(σ)\sqrt{L/c(\sigma)}cancels theC/(σ​nS)C/(\sigma n_{S})multiplier to leave the external quantityϱ⋆=L/(2​T​K​κS,γ,α)\varrho^{\star}=\sqrt{L/(2TK\,\kappa_{S,\gamma,\alpha})}withκS,γ,α:=γ​(γ+1)(S​γ+α)​(S​γ+α+1)\kappa_{S,\gamma,\alpha}\;:=\;\frac{\gamma(\gamma+1)}{(S\gamma+\alpha)(S\gamma+\alpha+1)}. If the integer-order restriction is enforced literally, takeλ^⋆=⌈λ⋆⌉\hat{\lambda}^{\star}=\lceil\lambda^{\star}\rceil. Thenϱ​(λ^⋆;σ,nS)=ϱ​(λ⋆;σ,nS)+q​(λ^⋆−λ⋆)​C/(σ​nS)\varrho(\hat{\lambda}^{\star};\sigma,n_{S})=\varrho(\lambda^{\star};\sigma,n_{S})+q(\hat{\lambda}^{\star}-\lambda^{\star})C/(\sigma n_{S}), so rounding adds at most the already controlled termq​C/(σ​nS)qC/(\sigma n_{S}). The corresponding absolute slack on the right-hand side of (S47) isc​(σ)​λ⋆​Δλ⋆=Crem​(c+c​(σ)​L)​(RΦ​(σ,nS;C)+q​Cσ​nS+ϱ⋆),\displaystyle c(\sigma)\lambda^{\star}\Delta_{\lambda^{\star}}=C_{\mathrm{rem}}(c+\sqrt{c(\sigma)L})(R_{\Phi}(\sigma,n_{S};C)+\tfrac{qC}{\sigma n_{S}}+\varrho^{\star}),

and dividing byεlead\varepsilon_{\mathrm{lead}},c​(σ)​λ⋆​Δλ⋆εlead≤Crem​(RΦ​(σ,nS;C)+ϱ⋆+q​Cσ​nS).\displaystyle\frac{c(\sigma)\lambda^{\star}\Delta_{\lambda^{\star}}}{\varepsilon_{\mathrm{lead}}}\leq C_{\mathrm{rem}}\Bigl(R_{\Phi}(\sigma,n_{S};C)+\varrho^{\star}+\frac{qC}{\sigma n_{S}}\Bigr).

Thus the dropped slack is asymptotically negligible only under the two small-o expansion requirementsϱ⋆=o​(1)\varrho^{\star}=o(1)andC/(σ​nS)=o​(1)C/(\sigma n_{S})=o(1), since the latter also givesRΦ​(σ,nS;C)=o​(1)R_{\Phi}(\sigma,n_{S};C)=o(1)andq​C/(σ​nS)=o​(1)qC/(\sigma n_{S})=o(1). Separately, the reduced Mironov objective is kept in the square-root regime by the constant-order regime conditionc​(σ)≤Lc(\sigma)\leq L. We therefore useϱ⋆≪1,Cσ​nS≪1,c​(σ)≤L,\displaystyle\varrho^{\star}\ll 1,\qquad\frac{C}{\sigma n_{S}}\ll 1,\qquad c(\sigma)\leq L,(S50)

When the RDP-to-DP bound is optimized overλ\lambda, the expansion is invoked only at the selected order (and, if needed, at its integer rounding). Thusϱ=o​(1)\varrho=o(1)means the selected-order conditionϱ​(λ⋆;σ,nS)=o​(1)\varrho(\lambda^{\star};\sigma,n_{S})=o(1), equivalentlyϱ​(λ^⋆;σ,nS)=o​(1)\varrho(\hat{\lambda}^{\star};\sigma,n_{S})=o(1)under the preceding rounding bound. It is not a uniform assertion over allλ\lambdain the infimum. In (S50), theϱ⋆\varrho^{\star}condition is external to the noise calibration and is exactly the first regime condition of Theorem2.1. The conditionC/(σ​nS)≪1C/(\sigma n_{S})\ll 1asks the added noise to dominate the per-record scaleC/nSC/n_{S}. The regime condition is left in the exact formc​(σ)≤Lc(\sigma)\leq Lrather than being converted into a separate noise scale, since that conversion only hides constants. Thus the leading-order calibration is justified for anyσ\sigmasatisfyingσ≳C​qnS​T​K​L​κS,γ,αϵ,c​(σ)≤L,CnS=o​(σ).\displaystyle\sigma\gtrsim\frac{Cq}{n_{S}}\frac{\sqrt{TKL\kappa_{S,\gamma,\alpha}}}{\epsilon},\qquad c(\sigma)\leq L,\qquad\frac{C}{n_{S}}=o(\sigma).(S51)

Since the finite accountant is monotone decreasing inσ\sigma, once these conditions hold at some boundary scaleσ0\sigma_{0}, anyσ≥σ0\sigma\geq\sigma_{0}also satisfies the privacy bound.

## S2.3.1Privacy Amplification from Dirichlet Randomness (Remark2.2)

We compare two leading-order calibrations that follow the same privacy accounting chain in SectionsS2.2.1-S2.3. The difference is how the record-level block weight is handled. The Dirichlet-aware accountant integrates over the actual law of the block weight, whereas the worst-case accountant ignores this law and uses only the deterministic envelopew¯i≤1/nS\bar{w}_{i}\leq 1/n_{S}.

## Dirichlet moment versus worst-case envelope.

Fix the possibly changed record, written as recordnnwithout loss of generality. Under theα=0\alpha=0blocked bootstrap used in the main text,w¯n=uπ​(n)nS,uπ​(n)∼Beta​(γ,(S−1)​γ).\bar{w}_{n}=\frac{u_{\pi(n)}}{n_{S}},\qquad u_{\pi(n)}\sim\text{Beta}(\gamma,(S-1)\gamma).

Hence𝔼Dir​[w¯n2]=1nS2​γ​(γ+1)S​γ​(S​γ+1)=κS,γnS2,κS,γ:=γ+1S​(S​γ+1).{\mathbb{E}}_{\mathrm{Dir}}[\bar{w}_{n}^{2}]=\frac{1}{n_{S}^{2}}\frac{\gamma(\gamma+1)}{S\gamma(S\gamma+1)}=\frac{\kappa_{S,\gamma}}{n_{S}^{2}},\qquad\kappa_{S,\gamma}:=\frac{\gamma+1}{S(S\gamma+1)}.

By contrast, the worst-case calibration does not assign a second moment tow¯n\bar{w}_{n}. It replaces the random weight by the deterministic upper boundw¯n2≤1nS2.\bar{w}_{n}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{n_{S}^{2}}.

Thus the comparison is between the actual Dirichlet second momentκS,γ/nS2\kappa_{S,\gamma}/n_{S}^{2}and the worst-case envelope1/nS21/n_{S}^{2}. The leading coefficient is therefore reduced by the factorκS,γ\kappa_{S,\gamma}when the Dirichlet randomness is used.

## Propagation to the leading privacy scale.

At leading order, (S38) gives𝔼w¯​[Φλ​(w¯)]=2​λ​(λ−1)​(C2/σ2)​𝔼​[w¯n2]​{1+RΦ​(σ,nS;C)}.{\mathbb{E}}_{\bar{w}}[\Phi_{\lambda}(\bar{w})]=2\lambda(\lambda-1)(C^{2}/\sigma^{2}){\mathbb{E}}[\bar{w}_{n}^{2}]\{1+R_{\Phi}(\sigma,n_{S};C)\}.

Accordingly, the slope rate in (S46) takes the formc∙​(σ)=2​T​K​q2​C2​m∙σ2​nS2,mDir=κS,γ,mworst=1.c_{\bullet}(\sigma)=\frac{2TKq^{2}C^{2}m_{\bullet}}{\sigma^{2}n_{S}^{2}},\qquad m_{\mathrm{Dir}}=\kappa_{S,\gamma},\qquad m_{\mathrm{worst}}=1.

The RDP-to-DP conversion in (S47) gives the reduced leading objectiveεlead,∙=c∙​(σ)+2​c∙​(σ)​L,L:=log⁡(1/δ).\varepsilon_{\mathrm{lead},\bullet}=c_{\bullet}(\sigma)+2\sqrt{c_{\bullet}(\sigma)L},\qquad L:=\log(1/\delta).

In the square-root regimec∙​(σ)≤Lc_{\bullet}(\sigma)\leq L, requiringεlead,∙≲ϵ\varepsilon_{\mathrm{lead},\bullet}\lesssim\epsilonyieldsΣ0,∙≍C​q​T​K​L​m∙nS​ϵ.\Sigma_{0,\bullet}\asymp\frac{Cq\sqrt{TKL\,m_{\bullet}}}{n_{S}\epsilon}.

ThereforeΣ0,Dir≍C​q​T​K​L​κS,γnS​ϵ,Σ0,worst≍C​q​T​K​LnS​ϵ,\Sigma_{0,\mathrm{Dir}}\asymp\frac{Cq\sqrt{TKL\,\kappa_{S,\gamma}}}{n_{S}\epsilon},\qquad\Sigma_{0,\mathrm{worst}}\asymp\frac{Cq\sqrt{TKL}}{n_{S}\epsilon},

and the leading noise-scale ratio isΣ0,DirΣ0,worst≍κS,γ.\frac{\Sigma_{0,\mathrm{Dir}}}{\Sigma_{0,\mathrm{worst}}}\asymp\sqrt{\kappa_{S,\gamma}}.

This is the amplification effect stated in Remark2.2.

Notably, the comparison above is a leading-order comparison and is valid only when the same expansion conditions used in Theorem2.1hold for the calibration under consideration. Withm∙∈{κS,γ,1}m_{\bullet}\in\{\kappa_{S,\gamma},1\}, the selected-order slack in Step 4 becomesϱ∙⋆=L2​T​K​m∙.\varrho^{\star}_{\bullet}=\sqrt{\frac{L}{2TK\,m_{\bullet}}}.

Thus the Dirichlet-aware calibration requiresL/(2​T​K​κS,γ)≪1\sqrt{L/(2TK\kappa_{S,\gamma})}\ll 1, while the worst-case calibration requiresL/(2​T​K)≪1\sqrt{L/(2TK)}\ll 1. SinceκS,γ≤1\kappa_{S,\gamma}\leq 1, the former is the more restrictive selected-order condition.

The local expansion also requiresC/(σ​nS)≪1C/(\sigma n_{S})\ll 1. Evaluated at the boundary scaleΣ0,∙\Sigma_{0,\bullet},CΣ0,∙​nS≍ϵq​T​K​L​m∙.\frac{C}{\Sigma_{0,\bullet}n_{S}}\asymp\frac{\epsilon}{q\sqrt{TKL\,m_{\bullet}}}.

Hence the Dirichlet-aware version is larger by the factor1/κS,γ1/\sqrt{\kappa_{S,\gamma}}relative to the worst-case version, again because it uses a smaller noise scale. Finally, the square-root regime conditionc∙​(σ)≤Lc_{\bullet}(\sigma)\leq Lreduces atσ=Σ0,∙\sigma=\Sigma_{0,\bullet}to the same requirementϵ≲L\epsilon\lesssim Lfor both calibrations. The sampling-ratio conditionq​log⁡(1/δ)(1−q)​ϵ=O​(1)\frac{q\log(1/\delta)}{(1-q)\epsilon}=O(1)

does not involveκS,γ\kappa_{S,\gamma}and is therefore identical in the two comparisons.

## S2.3.2Privacy-Side Effect of Pseudo-Samples

The analysis in SectionS2.3is written for the general posterior-bootstrap setup withα>0\alpha>0(Fonget al.2019, O’Hagan and Ročková2025). Suppose that, in addition to the private observationsX1,…,XnX_{1},\dots,X_{n}, we have data-independent pseudo-samplesX~1,…,X~n~∼Fbase\tilde{X}_{1},\dots,\tilde{X}_{\tilde{n}}\sim F_{\mathrm{base}}whereFbaseF_{\mathrm{base}}is a pre-specified distribution. Partition the private observations intoSSequal-sized blocks and also the pseudo-samples intoS~\tilde{S}equal-sized blocks, with sizesnS=n/Sn_{S}=n/Sandn~S~=n~/S~\tilde{n}_{\tilde{S}}=\tilde{n}/\tilde{S}. Draw(𝐮,𝐮~)∼Dirichlet​(γ​𝟏S,α​𝟏S~/S~)({\bf u},\tilde{\bf u})\sim\text{Dirichlet}(\gamma{\bf 1}_{S},\alpha{\bf 1}_{\tilde{S}}/\tilde{S})and setw¯i=uπ​(i)/nS\bar{w}_{i}=u_{\pi(i)}/n_{S}andw~¯j=u~π~​(j)/n~S~\bar{\tilde{w}}_{j}=\tilde{u}_{\tilde{\pi}(j)}/\tilde{n}_{\tilde{S}}. The amortized objective for the posterior bootstrap extends (2) tominG∈𝒢⁡𝔼(𝐮,𝐮~)​[∑i=1nw¯i​l​(G​(𝐮,𝐮~),Xi)+∑j=1n~w~¯j​l​(G​(𝐮,𝐮~),X~j)].\min_{G\in\mathcal{G}}{\mathbb{E}}_{({\bf u},\tilde{\bf u})}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bar{w}_{i}\,l(G({\bf u},\tilde{\bf u}),X_{i})+\sum_{j=1}^{\tilde{n}}\bar{\tilde{w}}_{j}\,l(G({\bf u},\tilde{\bf u}),\tilde{X}_{j})\right].

Forα=0\alpha=0, the pseudo-sample part is omitted and this reduces to the blocked Bayesian-bootstrap objective used in the main text.

The pseudo-samples are treated here as fixed independently of the private database. If they were generated from private data, their generation would need its own privacy accounting. Under this data-independent interpretation, the pseudo-samples affect the privacy analysis only through the Dirichlet allocation. For a private record,uπ​(i)∼Beta​(γ,(S−1)​γ+α)u_{\pi(i)}\sim\text{Beta}(\gamma,(S-1)\gamma+\alpha), and hence𝔼​[w¯i2]=γ​(γ+1)(S​γ+α)​(S​γ+α+1)​nS2=κS,γ,αnS2,κS,γ,α:=γ​(γ+1)(S​γ+α)​(S​γ+α+1).{\mathbb{E}}[\bar{w}_{i}^{2}]=\frac{\gamma(\gamma+1)}{(S\gamma+\alpha)(S\gamma+\alpha+1)n_{S}^{2}}=\frac{\kappa_{S,\gamma,\alpha}}{n_{S}^{2}},\qquad\kappa_{S,\gamma,\alpha}:=\frac{\gamma(\gamma+1)}{(S\gamma+\alpha)(S\gamma+\alpha+1)}.

Thus the safe scale in (S51) carriesσ∝κS,γ,α\sigma\propto\sqrt{\kappa_{S,\gamma,\alpha}}. SinceκS,γ,α\kappa_{S,\gamma,\alpha}decreases withα\alpha, larger prior concentration can reduce the privacy noise required for a fixed(ϵ,δ)(\epsilon,\delta). This privacy-side gain comes with statistical shrinkage towardFbaseF_{\mathrm{base}}, soα>0\alpha>0trades lower privacy noise against possible bias.

## Poisson variance.

Condition on𝒢t\mathcal{G}_{t}, under which the weightsw¯j(k)\bar{w}_{j}^{(k)}and scoresψj​(𝐮(k),ϕt)\psi_{j}({\bf u}^{(k)},\phi_{t})are deterministic. The Poisson indicators{Ij(k)}j\{I_{j}^{(k)}\}_{j}are independentBernoulli​(q)\mathrm{Bernoulli}(q), so the innerjj-sum has conditional second moment𝔼​[‖∑j(Ij(k)−q)​w¯j(k)​ψj‖2|𝒢t]=q​(1−q)​∑j=1n(w¯j(k))2​‖ψj​(𝐮(k),ϕt)‖2.\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}\Bigl[\|\sum_{j}(I_{j}^{(k)}-q)\bar{w}_{j}^{(k)}\psi_{j}\|^{2}\big|\mathcal{G}_{t}\Bigr]=q(1-q)\sum_{j=1}^{n}(\bar{w}_{j}^{(k)})^{2}\|\psi_{j}({\bf u}^{(k)},\phi_{t})\|^{2}.

Using‖ψj‖≤β1​C\|\psi_{j}\|\leq\beta_{1}Cfrom the Jacobian boundβ1\beta_{1}and the within-group constancyw¯j=uπ​(j)/nS\bar{w}_{j}=u_{\pi(j)}/n_{S}(so∑jw¯j2=nS−1​∑sus2=(S/n)​∑sus2\sum_{j}\bar{w}_{j}^{2}=n_{S}^{-1}\sum_{s}u_{s}^{2}=(S/n)\sum_{s}u_{s}^{2}),𝔼𝐮​[∑jw¯j2​‖ψj‖2]≤β12​C2​𝔼𝐮​[∑jw¯j2]=β12​C2​Sn​𝔼𝐮​[∑s=1Sus2].\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bf u}}\Bigl[\sum_{j}\bar{w}_{j}^{2}\|\psi_{j}\|^{2}\Bigr]\leq\beta_{1}^{2}C^{2}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bf u}}\Bigl[\sum_{j}\bar{w}_{j}^{2}\Bigr]=\beta_{1}^{2}C^{2}\frac{S}{n}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bf u}}\Bigl[\sum_{s=1}^{S}u_{s}^{2}\Bigr].

The Dirichlet second-moment identity (for𝐮∼Dirichlet​(γ​𝟏S){\bf u}\sim\text{Dirichlet}(\gamma\mathbf{1}_{S}),𝔼​[us2]=Var⁡(us)+(𝔼​us)2=S−1S2​(S​γ+1)+1S2{\mathbb{E}}[u_{s}^{2}]=\operatorname{Var}(u_{s})+({\mathbb{E}}u_{s})^{2}=\tfrac{S-1}{S^{2}(S\gamma+1)}+\tfrac{1}{S^{2}}, hence𝔼​[∑sus2]=γ+1S​γ+1{\mathbb{E}}[\sum_{s}u_{s}^{2}]=\tfrac{\gamma+1}{S\gamma+1}) then gives𝔼𝐮​[∑j=1nw¯j2]=(γ+1)​Sn​(S​γ+1).\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bf u}}\Bigl[\sum_{j=1}^{n}\bar{w}_{j}^{2}\Bigr]=\frac{(\gamma+1)S}{n(S\gamma+1)}.(S56)

Combining the conditional variance with (S56) and the1/K2⋅K1/K^{2}\cdot K-averaging over theKKindependent replicates yields the Poisson bound in (S54).

## Monte Carlo variance.

WriteZk:=∑j=1nw¯j(k)​ψj​(𝐮(k),ϕt)Z_{k}:=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\bar{w}_{j}^{(k)}\psi_{j}({\bf u}^{(k)},\phi_{t}), so thatξtMC=qK​∑k=1K(Zk−∇ℒ​(ϕt;𝐗1:n,π))\xi_{t}^{\rm MC}=\frac{q}{K}\sum_{k=1}^{K}(Z_{k}-\nabla\mathcal{L}(\phi_{t};{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi))with∇ℒ​(ϕt;𝐗1:n,π)=𝔼𝐮​Zk\nabla\mathcal{L}(\phi_{t};{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi)={\mathbb{E}}_{\bf u}Z_{k}by (S52). TheZkZ_{k}are i.i.d. acrosskk(fresh𝐮(k){\bf u}^{(k)}), so by independence and centring,𝔼​[‖ξtMC‖2∣ℱt]=q2K2​∑k=1K𝔼​‖Zk−∇ℒ‖2=q2K​Var𝐮⁡(Z1).\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\|\xi_{t}^{\rm MC}\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t}]=\frac{q^{2}}{K^{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{K}{\mathbb{E}}\|Z_{k}-\nabla\mathcal{L}\|^{2}=\frac{q^{2}}{K}\operatorname{Var}_{\bf u}(Z_{1}).

NoteVar𝐮⁡(Z1)≤𝔼​‖Z1‖2≤β12​C2\operatorname{Var}_{\bf u}(Z_{1})\leq{\mathbb{E}}\|Z_{1}\|^{2}\leq\beta_{1}^{2}C^{2}because of‖Z1‖≤∑jw¯j​‖ψj‖≤β1​C​∑jw¯j=β1​C\|Z_{1}\|\leq\sum_{j}\bar{w}_{j}\|\psi_{j}\|\leq\beta_{1}C\sum_{j}\bar{w}_{j}=\beta_{1}C.

## Lemma S2.13.

Suppose the conditions of Theorem3.1hold. By LemmaS2.12, the iteratesϕt+1=ϕt−η​g^t​(ϕt)\phi_{t+1}=\phi_{t}-\eta\hat{g}_{t}(\phi_{t})satisfy𝔼​[(ℒ​(ϕt+1;𝐗1:n,π)−ℒΦ,𝐗1:n,π⋆)|ℱt]≤(1−η​q​μℒ)​(ℒ​(ϕt;𝐗1:n,π)−ℒΦ,𝐗1:n,π⋆)+Mℒ​η22​τ2,\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[(\mathcal{L}(\phi_{t+1};{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi)-\mathcal{L}^{\star}_{\Phi,{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi})\big|\mathcal{F}_{t}]\leq(1-\eta q\mu_{\mathcal{L}})(\mathcal{L}(\phi_{t};{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi)-\mathcal{L}^{\star}_{\Phi,{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi})+\frac{M_{\mathcal{L}}\eta^{2}}{2}\tau^{2},(S57)

whereτ2\tau^{2}is an upper bound of𝔼​[‖ξt‖2∣ℱt]=𝔼​[‖ξtMC‖2∣ℱt]+𝔼​[‖ξtPois‖2∣ℱt]+𝔼​[‖ξtDP‖2∣ℱt]{\mathbb{E}}[\|\xi_{t}\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t}]={\mathbb{E}}[\|\xi_{t}^{\rm MC}\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t}]+{\mathbb{E}}[\|\xi_{t}^{\rm Pois}\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t}]+{\mathbb{E}}[\|\xi_{t}^{\rm DP}\|^{2}\mid\mathcal{F}_{t}]by (S54)-(S55).

## Proof.

SinceMℒ≤β12​K2+β2​C<∞M_{\mathcal{L}}\leq\beta_{1}^{2}K_{2}+\beta_{2}C<\infty,ℒ​(ϕ;𝐗1:n,π)\mathcal{L}(\phi;{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi)is anMℒM_{\cal L}-smooth function over the convex domainΦ\Phifor any given𝐗1:n,π{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi. Thus, the descent lemma givesℒ​(ϕt+1;𝐗1:n,π)≤ℒ​(ϕt;𝐗1:n,π)+⟨∇ℒ​(ϕt;𝐗1:n,π),ϕt+1−ϕt⟩+Mℒ2​‖ϕt+1−ϕt‖2\mathcal{L}(\phi_{t+1};{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi)\leq\mathcal{L}(\phi_{t};{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi)+\langle\nabla\mathcal{L}(\phi_{t};{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi),\phi_{t+1}-\phi_{t}\rangle+\tfrac{M_{\mathcal{L}}}{2}\|\phi_{t+1}-\phi_{t}\|^{2}. Substituteϕt+1−ϕt=−η​(q​∇ℒ​(ϕt;𝐗1:n,π)+ξt)\phi_{t+1}-\phi_{t}=-\eta(q\nabla\mathcal{L}(\phi_{t};{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi)+\xi_{t})and take𝔼[⋅∣ℱt]{\mathbb{E}}[\cdot\mid\mathcal{F}_{t}]. The linear cross term vanishes by LemmaS2.12, and𝔼​[‖q​∇ℒ​(ϕt;𝐗1:n,π)+ξt‖2|ℱt]=q2​‖∇ℒ​(ϕt;𝐗1:n,π)‖2+τt2.\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\|q\nabla\mathcal{L}(\phi_{t};{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi)+\xi_{t}\|^{2}|\mathcal{F}_{t}]=q^{2}\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\phi_{t};{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi)\|^{2}+\tau_{t}^{2}.

whereτt2\tau_{t}^{2}has the uniform upper boundτ2\tau^{2}from LemmaS2.12. Then we have𝔼​[ℒ​(ϕt+1;𝐗1:n,π)∣ℱt]≤ℒ​(ϕt;𝐗1:n,π)−η​q​(1−η​q​Mℒ/2)​‖∇ℒ​(ϕt;𝐗1:n,π)‖2+(Mℒ​η2/2)​τ2{\mathbb{E}}[\mathcal{L}(\phi_{t+1};{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi)\mid\mathcal{F}_{t}]\leq\mathcal{L}(\phi_{t};{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi)-\eta q(1-\eta qM_{\mathcal{L}}/2)\|\nabla\mathcal{L}(\phi_{t};{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi)\|^{2}+(M_{\mathcal{L}}\eta^{2}/2)\tau^{2}and subtractℒΦ,𝐗1:n,π⋆\mathcal{L}^{\star}_{\Phi,{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi}in both sides. The step-size choiceη≤1/(q​Mℒ)\eta\leq 1/(qM_{\mathcal{L}})gives1−η​q​Mℒ/2≥1/21-\eta qM_{\mathcal{L}}/2\geq 1/2, and the PL condition leads to−η​q​(1−η​q​Mℒ/2)​‖∇ℒ‖2≤−η​q​μℒ​(ℒ​(ϕt;𝐗1:n,π)−ℒΦ,𝐗1:n,π⋆)-\eta q(1-\eta qM_{\mathcal{L}}/2)\|\nabla\mathcal{L}\|^{2}\leq-\eta q\mu_{\mathcal{L}}(\mathcal{L}(\phi_{t};{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi)-\mathcal{L}^{\star}_{\Phi,{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi}).

## Lemma S2.14.

Assume (A1-5). Letℰ:=𝒢n∩E~nBlock\mathcal{E}:=\mathcal{G}_{n}\cap\tilde{E}_{n}^{\text{Block}}with𝒢n\mathcal{G}_{n}andE~nBlock\tilde{E}_{n}^{\text{Block}}as in LemmasS2.6andS2.7, and adopt the LemmaS2.7notationθBlock:=θBlock⋆​(𝐰¯)\theta^{\text{Block}}:=\theta^{\star}_{\text{Block}}(\bar{\bf w}),μ:=μ∞/2\mu:=\mu_{\infty}/2, andrrthe localization radius of LemmaS2.6. Then, it follows that
- (i)

onℰ\mathcal{E}, for the realized block weight vector𝐰¯=𝐰¯​(𝐮)\bar{\bf w}=\bar{\bf w}({\bf u})and everyθ∈Θ\theta\in\Theta,L​(θ;𝐰¯)−L​(θBlock;𝐰¯)≥{μ4​‖θ−θBlock‖2,‖θ−θBlock‖≤r/2,μ​r8​‖θ−θBlock‖,‖θ−θBlock‖>r/2.\displaystyle L(\theta;\bar{\bf w})-L(\theta^{\text{Block}};\bar{\bf w})\geq\begin{cases}\dfrac{\mu}{4}\|\theta-\theta^{\text{Block}}\|^{2},&\|\theta-\theta^{\text{Block}}\|\leq r/2,\\[8.0pt]
\dfrac{\mu r}{8}\|\theta-\theta^{\text{Block}}\|,&\|\theta-\theta^{\text{Block}}\|>r/2.\end{cases}(S58)
- (ii)

for everyθ∈Θ\theta\in\Theta,𝔼​[𝟏ℰ​‖θ−θBlock‖2]≤4μ​(1+2​Dr)​𝔼​[𝟏ℰ​(L​(θ;𝐰¯)−L​(θBlock;𝐰¯))],\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}[\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{E}}\|\theta-\theta^{\text{Block}}\|^{2}]\leq\frac{4}{\mu}\left(1+\frac{2D}{r}\right){\mathbb{E}}[\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{E}}(L(\theta;\bar{\bf w})-L(\theta^{\text{Block}};\bar{\bf w}))],(S59)

whereDΘ:=diam⁡(Θ)<∞D_{\Theta}:=\operatorname{diam}(\Theta)<\inftyby (A2).

## Proof.

We first prove the deterministic growth bound (S58) onℰ\mathcal{E}and then deduce the expectation bound (S59) by a two-regime split.

## Setup on the good event.

By LemmaS2.7, applied at concentrationγ\gamma, onℰ\mathcal{E}the weighted minimizerθBlock\theta^{\text{Block}}satisfies the quantitative bound‖θBlock−θ0⋆‖≤r/2\|\theta^{\text{Block}}-\theta_{0}^{\star}\|\leq r/2, sharper than theB​(θ0⋆,r)¯\overline{B(\theta_{0}^{\star},r)}containment. By LemmaS2.7, the weighted Hessian satisfies∇θ2L​(θ;𝐰¯)⪰(μ/2)​I\nabla_{\theta}^{2}L(\theta;\bar{\bf w})\succeq(\mu/2)Ifor everyθ∈B​(θ0⋆,r)¯\theta\in\overline{B(\theta_{0}^{\star},r)}. The triangle inequality then givesB​(θBlock,r/2)¯⊂B​(θ0⋆,r)¯\overline{B(\theta^{\text{Block}},r/2)}\subset\overline{B(\theta_{0}^{\star},r)}, so the weighted Hessian is bounded below by(μ/2)​I(\mu/2)IthroughoutB​(θBlock,r/2)¯\overline{B(\theta^{\text{Block}},r/2)}.

## Inside the ball.

Fixθ∈B​(θBlock,r/2)¯\theta\in\overline{B(\theta^{\text{Block}},r/2)}. The segment fromθBlock\theta^{\text{Block}}toθ\thetalies in this ball, so the mean-value form of second-order Taylor’s theorem yields someξ\xion the segment withL​(θ;𝐰¯)=L​(θBlock;𝐰¯)+∇θL​(θBlock;𝐰¯)⊤​(θ−θBlock)+12​(θ−θBlock)⊤​H​(ξ;𝐰¯)​(θ−θBlock).\displaystyle L(\theta;\bar{\bf w})=L(\theta^{\text{Block}};\bar{\bf w})+\nabla_{\theta}L(\theta^{\text{Block}};\bar{\bf w})^{\top}(\theta-\theta^{\text{Block}})+\tfrac{1}{2}(\theta-\theta^{\text{Block}})^{\top}H(\xi;\bar{\bf w})(\theta-\theta^{\text{Block}}).

where the first term disappears by the first-order condition. The Hessian PD boundH​(ξ;𝐰¯)⪰(μ/2)​IH(\xi;\bar{\bf w})\succeq(\mu/2)Iat thisξ\xigives the first branch of (S58):L​(θ;𝐰¯)−L​(θBlock;𝐰¯)≥μ4​‖θ−θBlock‖2.\displaystyle L(\theta;\bar{\bf w})-L(\theta^{\text{Block}};\bar{\bf w})\geq\tfrac{\mu}{4}\|\theta-\theta^{\text{Block}}\|^{2}.(S60)

## Outside the ball.

Fixθ∈Θ\theta\in\Thetawith‖θ−θBlock‖>r/2\|\theta-\theta^{\text{Block}}\|>r/2, and sett:=(r/2)/‖θ−θBlock‖∈(0,1)t:=(r/2)/\|\theta-\theta^{\text{Block}}\|\in(0,1). The pointθt:=θBlock+t​(θ−θBlock)\theta_{t}:=\theta^{\text{Block}}+t(\theta-\theta^{\text{Block}})lies on the boundary∂B​(θBlock,r/2)\partial B(\theta^{\text{Block}},r/2), so (S60) applied atθt\theta_{t}givesL​(θt;𝐰¯)−L​(θBlock;𝐰¯)≥(μ/4)​(r/2)2=μ​r2/16.L(\theta_{t};\bar{\bf w})-L(\theta^{\text{Block}};\bar{\bf w})\geq(\mu/4)(r/2)^{2}=\mu r^{2}/16.By (A4),L​(⋅;𝐰¯)L(\cdot;\bar{\bf w})is convex onΘ\Theta, so the restrictions↦L​(θBlock+s​(θ−θBlock);𝐰¯)s\mapsto L(\theta^{\text{Block}}+s(\theta-\theta^{\text{Block}});\bar{\bf w})tos∈[0,1]s\in[0,1]is convex with valueL​(θBlock;𝐰¯)L(\theta^{\text{Block}};\bar{\bf w})ats=0s=0. The standard chord inequalityf​(s)≤s​f​(1)+(1−s)​f​(0)f(s)\leq sf(1)+(1-s)f(0)applied ats=ts=tgivesL​(θt;𝐰¯)−L​(θBlock;𝐰¯)≤t​(L​(θ;𝐰¯)−L​(θBlock;𝐰¯)),L(\theta_{t};\bar{\bf w})-L(\theta^{\text{Block}};\bar{\bf w})\leq t(L(\theta;\bar{\bf w})-L(\theta^{\text{Block}};\bar{\bf w})),soL​(θ;𝐰¯)−L​(θBlock;𝐰¯)≥1t⋅μ​r216=μ​r8​‖θ−θBlock‖,\displaystyle L(\theta;\bar{\bf w})-L(\theta^{\text{Block}};\bar{\bf w})\geq\frac{1}{t}\cdot\frac{\mu r^{2}}{16}=\frac{\mu r}{8}\|\theta-\theta^{\text{Block}}\|,(S61)

which is the second branch of (S58).

## From growth to expected squared norm.

For a randomθ∈Θ\theta\in\Theta, split on the eventA:={‖θ−θBlock‖≤r/2}A:=\{\|\theta-\theta^{\text{Block}}\|\leq r/2\}. OnAA, inverting (S60),‖θ−θBlock‖2≤(4/μ)​(L​(θ;𝐰¯)−L​(θBlock;𝐰¯)).\|\theta-\theta^{\text{Block}}\|^{2}\leq(4/\mu)(L(\theta;\bar{\bf w})-L(\theta^{\text{Block}};\bar{\bf w})).OnAcA^{c}, inverting (S61),‖θ−θBlock‖≤(8/(μ​r))​(L​(θ;𝐰¯)−L​(θBlock;𝐰¯)).\|\theta-\theta^{\text{Block}}\|\leq(8/(\mu r))(L(\theta;\bar{\bf w})-L(\theta^{\text{Block}};\bar{\bf w})).Sinceθ,θBlock∈Θ\theta,\theta^{\text{Block}}\in\Thetaanddiam⁡(Θ)=DΘ\operatorname{diam}(\Theta)=D_{\Theta},‖θ−θBlock‖≤DΘ\|\theta-\theta^{\text{Block}}\|\leq D_{\Theta}, hence‖θ−θBlock‖2≤DΘ​‖θ−θBlock‖≤(8​DΘ/(μ​r))​(L​(θ;𝐰¯)−L​(θBlock;𝐰¯)).\|\theta-\theta^{\text{Block}}\|^{2}\leq D_{\Theta}\|\theta-\theta^{\text{Block}}\|\leq(8D_{\Theta}/(\mu r))(L(\theta;\bar{\bf w})-L(\theta^{\text{Block}};\bar{\bf w})).Adding the two pieces with the indicator𝟏ℰ\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{E}},𝟏ℰ​‖θ−θBlock‖2≤(4μ+8​Dμ​r)​𝟏ℰ​(L​(θ;𝐰¯)−L​(θBlock;𝐰¯)).\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{E}}\|\theta-\theta^{\text{Block}}\|^{2}\leq(\tfrac{4}{\mu}+\tfrac{8D}{\mu r})\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{E}}(L(\theta;\bar{\bf w})-L(\theta^{\text{Block}};\bar{\bf w})).

Taking expectations and factoring(4/μ)​(1+2​DΘ/r)(4/\mu)(1+2D_{\Theta}/r)gives (S59).

## S2.4.3Proof of Theorem3.1

The proof assembles the three lemmas (LemmasS2.12-S2.14).

## Unrolling the one-step descent.

Define theϕ\phi-space function-value excessut:=𝔼𝐗1:n,π,𝝎T​[ℒ​(ϕt;𝐗1:n,π)−ℒΦ,𝐗1:n,π⋆]u_{t}:={\mathbb{E}}_{{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi,\boldsymbol{\omega}_{T}}[\mathcal{L}(\phi_{t};{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi)-\mathcal{L}^{\star}_{\Phi,{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi}], and it is assumed that each update satisfiesϕt∈Φ\phi_{t}\in\Phifor everytt. Taking𝔼𝐗1:n,π,𝝎T​[⋅]{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi,\boldsymbol{\omega}_{T}}[\cdot]on both sides of (S57), we observeut+1≤ρ​ut+(Mℒ​η2/2)​τ2u_{t+1}\;\leq\;\rho u_{t}\;+\;(M_{\mathcal{L}}\eta^{2}/2)\tau^{2}withρ:=1−η​q​μℒ∈[0,1)\rho:=1-\eta q\mu_{\mathcal{L}}\in[0,1). By unrolling it,uT≤ρT​u0+Mℒ​η2​τ22​∑s=0T−1ρs≤ρT​𝔼𝐗1:n,π,ϕ0​(ℒ​(ϕ0)−ℒΦ,𝐗1:n,π⋆)+Mℒ​η​τ22​q​μℒ,\displaystyle u_{T}\leq\rho^{T}u_{0}+\tfrac{M_{\mathcal{L}}\eta^{2}\tau^{2}}{2}\sum_{s=0}^{T-1}\rho^{s}\leq\rho^{T}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi,\phi_{0}}(\mathcal{L}(\phi_{0})-\mathcal{L}^{\star}_{\Phi,{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi})+\tfrac{M_{\mathcal{L}}\eta\tau^{2}}{2q\mu_{\mathcal{L}}},(S62)

sinceu0=𝔼𝐗1:n,π,ϕ0​ℒ​(ϕ0)−ℒΦ,𝐗1:n,π⋆u_{0}={\mathbb{E}}_{{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi,\phi_{0}}\mathcal{L}(\phi_{0})-\mathcal{L}^{\star}_{\Phi,{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi}and∑s≥0ρs≤1/(1−ρ)=1/(η​q​μℒ)\sum_{s\geq 0}\rho^{s}\leq 1/(1-\rho)=1/(\eta q\mu_{\mathcal{L}}).

## Convertingϕ\phi-space intoθ\theta-space.

Define the function-value excess of the iterate against the weighted minimizer,ΔT:=𝔼𝐗1:n,π,𝝎T,𝐮​[L​(GϕT​(𝐮);𝐰¯​(𝐮))−L​(θBlock⋆​(𝐰¯​(𝐮));𝐰¯​(𝐮))],\displaystyle\Delta_{T}:={\mathbb{E}}_{{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi,\boldsymbol{\omega}_{T},{\bf u}}[L(G_{\phi_{T}}({\bf u});\bar{\bf w}({\bf u}))-L(\theta^{\star}_{\text{Block}}(\bar{\bf w}({\bf u}));\bar{\bf w}({\bf u}))],(S63)

where the expectation is over the training randomness𝝎T\boldsymbol{\omega}_{T}, the fresh draw𝐮{\bf u}, and the data(𝐗1:n,π)({\bf X}_{1:n},\pi). The integrand in (S63) is non-negative becauseθBlock⋆​(𝐰¯)\theta^{\star}_{\text{Block}}(\bar{\bf w})is by definition a global minimizer ofL​(⋅;𝐰¯)L(\cdot;\bar{\bf w})onΘ\Theta. Withℒopt,𝐗1:n,π:=𝔼𝐮​[L​(θBlock⋆​(𝐰¯​(𝐮));𝐰¯​(𝐮))]\mathcal{L}_{{\rm opt},{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi}:={\mathbb{E}}_{\bf u}[L(\theta^{\star}_{\text{Block}}(\bar{\bf w}({\bf u}));\bar{\bf w}({\bf u}))]andℒ​(ϕ;𝐗1:n,π):=𝔼𝐮​[L​(Gϕ​(𝐮);𝐰¯​(𝐮))]\mathcal{L}(\phi;{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi):={\mathbb{E}}_{\bf u}[L(G_{\phi}({\bf u});\bar{\bf w}({\bf u}))], the tower property givesΔT=𝔼𝐗1:n,π,𝝎T​[ℒ​(ϕT;𝐗1:n,π)−ℒopt,𝐗1:n,π]=𝔼​[uT]+εapp,\Delta_{T}={\mathbb{E}}_{{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi,\boldsymbol{\omega}_{T}}[\mathcal{L}(\phi_{T};{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi)-\mathcal{L}_{{\rm opt},{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi}]={\mathbb{E}}[u_{T}]+\varepsilon_{\rm app},whereεapp=𝔼𝐗1:n,π​[ℒΦ,𝐗1:n,π⋆−ℒopt,𝐗1:n,π]≥0\varepsilon_{\rm app}={\mathbb{E}}_{{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi}[\mathcal{L}^{\star}_{\Phi,{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi}-\mathcal{L}_{{\rm opt},{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi}]\geq 0is the network-class approximation gap of the in-class minimum to the per-realization target. Hence, (S62) leads toΔT≤ρT​𝔼𝐗1:n,π,ϕ0​[ℒ​(ϕ0;𝐗1:n,π)−ℒΦ,𝐗1:n,π⋆]+Mℒ​η​τ22​q​μℒ+εapp.\displaystyle\Delta_{T}\leq\rho^{T}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi,\phi_{0}}[\mathcal{L}(\phi_{0};{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi)-\mathcal{L}^{\star}_{\Phi,{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi}]+\tfrac{M_{\mathcal{L}}\eta\tau^{2}}{2q\mu_{\mathcal{L}}}+\varepsilon_{\rm app}.(S64)

Withθ=GϕT​(𝐮)∈Θ\theta=G_{\phi_{T}}({\bf u})\in\Theta, LemmaS2.14is applied to𝔼𝐗1:n,π,𝝎T,𝐮​[𝟏ℰ​‖GϕT​(𝐮)−θBlock⋆​(𝐰¯​(𝐮))‖2]≤CBlock​ΔT,\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi,\boldsymbol{\omega}_{T},{\bf u}}[\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{E}}\|G_{\phi_{T}}({\bf u})-\theta^{\star}_{\text{Block}}(\bar{\bf w}({\bf u}))\|^{2}]\leq C_{\text{Block}}\Delta_{T},(S65)

whereCBlock=(8/μ∞)​(1+2​DΘ/r)C_{\text{Block}}=(8/\mu_{\infty})(1+2D_{\Theta}/r).

## The bad setℰc\mathcal{E}^{c}.

On the complement eventℰc\mathcal{E}^{c}, bothGϕT​(𝐮)G_{\phi_{T}}({\bf u})andθBlock⋆​(𝐰¯​(𝐮))\theta^{\star}_{\text{Block}}(\bar{\bf w}({\bf u}))lie inΘ\Theta(by (A2) and the definition ofθBlock⋆\theta^{\star}_{\text{Block}}as a minimizer onΘ\Theta), so the diameter bound gives, pointwise,‖GϕT​(𝐮)−θBlock⋆​(𝐰¯​(𝐮))‖2≤DΘ2\|G_{\phi_{T}}({\bf u})-\theta^{\star}_{\text{Block}}(\bar{\bf w}({\bf u}))\|^{2}\leq D_{\Theta}^{2}. Therefore𝔼𝐗1:n,π,𝝎T,𝐮​[𝟏ℰc​‖GϕT​(𝐮)−θBlock⋆​(𝐰¯​(𝐮))‖2]≤DΘ2​ℙ​(ℰc).\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi,\boldsymbol{\omega}_{T},{\bf u}}[\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{E}^{c}}\|G_{\phi_{T}}({\bf u})-\theta^{\star}_{\text{Block}}(\bar{\bf w}({\bf u}))\|^{2}]\leq D_{\Theta}^{2}{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E}^{c}).(S66)

By LemmaS2.6the regularity event obeysℙ​(𝒢nc)=O​(e−c′​n){\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{G}_{n}^{c})=O(e^{-c^{\prime}n}). The localization thresholdsμn/4\mu_{n}/4andμn​r/4\mu_{n}r/4are fixed constants, so a fourth-moment Markov inequality with LemmaS2.5, which gives𝔼u,π​[‖gBlock‖4∣𝐗1:n]=O​(γ−3​n−2){\mathbb{E}}_{u,\pi}[\|g_{\text{Block}}\|^{4}\mid{\bf X}_{1:n}]=O(\gamma^{-3}n^{-2})and𝔼u,π​[‖BBlock‖F4∣𝐗1:n]=O​(γ−3​n−2){\mathbb{E}}_{u,\pi}[\|B_{\text{Block}}\|_{F}^{4}\mid{\bf X}_{1:n}]=O(\gamma^{-3}n^{-2})on𝒢n\mathcal{G}_{n}, yieldsℙ​(𝒢n∩E~nBlock,c)≤𝔼​[‖BBlock‖F4​𝟏𝒢n](μn/4)4+𝔼​[‖gBlock‖4​𝟏𝒢n](μn​r/4)4=O​(n−2).\displaystyle{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{G}_{n}\cap\tilde{E}_{n}^{\text{Block},c})\leq\frac{{\mathbb{E}}[\|B_{\text{Block}}\|_{F}^{4}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{G}_{n}}]}{(\mu_{n}/4)^{4}}+\frac{{\mathbb{E}}[\|g_{\text{Block}}\|^{4}\mathbf{1}_{\mathcal{G}_{n}}]}{(\mu_{n}r/4)^{4}}=O(n^{-2}).

Henceℙ​(ℰc)≤ℙ​(𝒢nc)+ℙ​(𝒢n∩E~nBlock,c)=O​(n−2){\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E}^{c})\leq{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{G}_{n}^{c})+{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{G}_{n}\cap\tilde{E}_{n}^{\text{Block},c})=O(n^{-2}). Summing (S65) and (S66) and substituting (S64),𝔼𝐗1:n,π,𝝎T,𝐮​‖GϕT​(𝐮)−θBlock⋆​(𝐰¯​(𝐮))‖2≤CBlock​ΔT+DΘ2​ℙ​(ℰc),\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi,\boldsymbol{\omega}_{T},{\bf u}}\|G_{\phi_{T}}({\bf u})-\theta^{\star}_{\text{Block}}(\bar{\bf w}({\bf u}))\|^{2}\leq C_{\text{Block}}\Delta_{T}+D_{\Theta}^{2}{\mathbb{P}}(\mathcal{E}^{c}),

which is (6).∎

## S2.5Proof of Corollary4.1

Recall that the variance is defined asVar⁡(Z):=𝔼​‖Z−𝔼​Z‖2\operatorname{Var}(Z):={\mathbb{E}}\|Z-{\mathbb{E}}Z\|^{2}.

## Lemma S2.15.

LetA,B∼PA,B\sim Pbe jointly distributed random vectors inℝd{\mathbb{R}}^{d}, taking a diameterDDwith finite second moments. Then|VarP⁡(A)−VarP⁡(B)|≤2​D​𝔼P​‖A−B‖≤2​D​(𝔼P​‖A−B‖2)1/2.\displaystyle\bigl|\operatorname{Var}_{P}(A)-\operatorname{Var}_{P}(B)\bigr|\leq 2D{\mathbb{E}}_{P}\|A-B\|\leq 2D\bigl({\mathbb{E}}_{P}\|A-B\|^{2}\bigr)^{1/2}.

## Proof.

Let(A′,B′)(A^{\prime},B^{\prime})be an independent copy of the pair(A,B)(A,B). Since𝔼​⟨Z−𝔼​Z,Z′−𝔼​Z⟩=0{\mathbb{E}}\langle Z-{\mathbb{E}}Z,Z^{\prime}-{\mathbb{E}}Z\rangle=0for an independent copyZ′Z^{\prime},Var⁡(Z)=12​𝔼​‖Z−Z′‖2\operatorname{Var}(Z)=\tfrac{1}{2}{\mathbb{E}}\|Z-Z^{\prime}\|^{2}holds. Thus,Var⁡(A)−Var⁡(B)=12​𝔼​[(‖A−A′‖−‖B−B′‖)​(‖A−A′‖+‖B−B′‖)].\displaystyle\operatorname{Var}(A)-\operatorname{Var}(B)=\tfrac{1}{2}{\mathbb{E}}\bigl[(\|A-A^{\prime}\|-\|B-B^{\prime}\|)(\|A-A^{\prime}\|+\|B-B^{\prime}\|)\bigr].

By the reverse triangle inequality applied toA−A′A-A^{\prime}andB−B′B-B^{\prime},|‖A−A′‖−‖B−B′‖|≤‖(A−A′)−(B−B′)‖=‖(A−B)−(A′−B′)‖≤‖A−B‖+‖A′−B′‖|\,\|A-A^{\prime}\|-\|B-B^{\prime}\|\,|\leq\|(A-A^{\prime})-(B-B^{\prime})\|=\|(A-B)-(A^{\prime}-B^{\prime})\|\leq\|A-B\|+\|A^{\prime}-B^{\prime}\|. Hence|Var⁡(A)−Var⁡(B)|≤D​(𝔼​‖A−B‖+𝔼​‖A′−B′‖)=2​D​𝔼​‖A−B‖|\operatorname{Var}(A)-\operatorname{Var}(B)|\leq D({\mathbb{E}}\|A-B\|+{\mathbb{E}}\|A^{\prime}-B^{\prime}\|)=2D\,{\mathbb{E}}\|A-B\|, and Jensen’s inequality gives the last bound.
∎

We split the deviation by inserting the ideal block target.

Step 1 (generator vs. block).Apply LemmaS2.15conditionally on(𝐗1:n,π,𝝎T)({\bf X}_{1:n},\pi,\boldsymbol{\omega}_{T})withA=GϕT(γ)​(𝐮)A=G_{\phi_{T}}^{(\gamma)}({\bf u})andB=θBlock⋆​(𝐰¯γ​(𝐮))B=\theta^{\star}_{\text{Block}}(\bar{\bf w}_{\gamma}({\bf u})), the variance taken over the fresh draw𝐮{\bf u}(with an independent copy𝐮′{\bf u}^{\prime}). Now, averaging over(𝐗1:n,π,𝝎T)({\bf X}_{1:n},\pi,\boldsymbol{\omega}_{T})via|𝔼(⋅)|≤𝔼|⋅||{\mathbb{E}}(\cdot)|\leq{\mathbb{E}}|\cdot|gives|𝔼𝐗1:n,π,𝝎T​Var𝐮⁡[GϕT(γ)]−𝔼𝐗1:n,π​Var𝐮⁡[θBlock⋆]|≤2​DΘ​𝔼𝐗1:n,π,𝝎T,𝐮​‖GϕT(γ)−θBlock⋆‖≤2​DΘ​ΔT​(γ)1/2,\displaystyle\bigl|{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi,\boldsymbol{\omega}_{T}}\operatorname{Var}_{\bf u}[G_{\phi_{T}}^{(\gamma)}]-{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi}\operatorname{Var}_{\bf u}[\theta^{\star}_{\text{Block}}]\bigr|\leq 2D_{\Theta}{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi,\boldsymbol{\omega}_{T},{\bf u}}\bigl\|G_{\phi_{T}}^{(\gamma)}-\theta^{\star}_{\text{Block}}\bigr\|\leq 2D_{\Theta}\Delta_{T}(\gamma)^{1/2},

whereΔT​(γ)=𝔼𝐗1:n,π,𝝎T,𝐮​‖GϕT(γ)−θBlock⋆‖2\Delta_{T}(\gamma)={\mathbb{E}}_{{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi,\boldsymbol{\omega}_{T},{\bf u}}\bigl\|G_{\phi_{T}}^{(\gamma)}-\theta^{\star}_{\text{Block}}\bigr\|^{2}.

Step 2 (triangle split).InsertingθBlock⋆\theta^{\star}_{\text{Block}},|𝔼𝐗1:n,π,𝝎T​Var𝐮⁡[GϕT(γ)]−𝔼𝐗1:n​Var𝐰⁡[θDirichlet⋆]|\displaystyle\bigl|{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi,\boldsymbol{\omega}_{T}}\operatorname{Var}_{\bf u}[G_{\phi_{T}}^{(\gamma)}]-{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bf X}_{1:n}}\operatorname{Var}_{\bf w}[\theta^{\star}_{\text{Dirichlet}}]\bigr|≤|𝔼𝐗1:n,π,𝝎T​Var𝐮⁡[GϕT(γ)]−𝔼𝐗1:n,π​Var𝐮⁡[θBlock⋆]|+|𝔼𝐗1:n,π​Var𝐮⁡[θBlock⋆]−𝔼𝐗1:n​Var𝐰⁡[θDirichlet⋆]|.\displaystyle\leq\bigl|{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi,\boldsymbol{\omega}_{T}}\operatorname{Var}_{\bf u}[G_{\phi_{T}}^{(\gamma)}]-{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi}\operatorname{Var}_{\bf u}[\theta^{\star}_{\text{Block}}]\bigr|+\bigl|{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi}\operatorname{Var}_{\bf u}[\theta^{\star}_{\text{Block}}]-{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bf X}_{1:n}}\operatorname{Var}_{\bf w}[\theta^{\star}_{\text{Dirichlet}}]\bigr|.

Therefore, the first term is handled by the quantity in Step 1.

Step 3Proposition4.1gives𝔼𝐗1:n,π​Var𝐮⁡[θBlock⋆]−𝔼𝐗1:n​Var𝐰⁡[θDirichlet⋆]=aS,n​(γ)​an+O​((1+γ−3/2)​n−3/2+γ−3​n−2).\displaystyle{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bf X}_{1:n},\pi}\operatorname{Var}_{\bf u}[\theta^{\star}_{\text{Block}}]-{\mathbb{E}}_{{\bf X}_{1:n}}\operatorname{Var}_{\bf w}[\theta^{\star}_{\text{Dirichlet}}]=a_{S,n}(\gamma)a_{n}+O((1+\gamma^{-3/2})n^{-3/2}+\gamma^{-3}n^{-2}).

By combining Steps 1-3, we have the statement. ∎

## S3Implementation Details

This section details the implementation of every method in the three simulation studies of Section5: the linear-regression study under heavy-tailed errors (Section5.1) and the exponential-family study (Section5.2).

## S3.1Competing Methods

We compare against three differentially private baselines. Each represents a major branch of private Bayesian or bootstrap inference. We briefly describe them and give the method-specific implementation in the subsections that follow. Refer to the original papers for more details.

## DP-SGLD.

Our customized differentially private stochastic-gradient Langevin dynamics privatizes the score at each Langevin step. Each update clips the per-sample gradient and adds Gaussian noise for privacy, and the iterates are taken as approximate posterior samples. The Langevin Gaussian noise decays byt−1/3t^{-1/3}following(Liet al.2019). We acknowledge that a single draw from DP-SGLD need not invite additional Gaussian noise for privacy under regularity conditions. However, for UQ with many draws, we perturb the gradient as a safeguard. Its privacy accountant has the same systematic structure as ours for fair comparison; the replace-one Rényi bound ofBirrellet al.(2024, Theorem C.9)is composed over theTTiterations and converted to(ϵ,δ)(\epsilon,\delta)-DP by the Asoodeh conversion of SectionS1.4.

## NoisyGibbs(Bernstein and Sheldon2018,2019).

It releases private sufficient statistics under the Gaussian mechanism, treats the noisy statistics as observed and the true statistics as latent, and runs a Gibbs sampler over the latent statistics and the parameters. The posterior draws are DP by post-processing. We useBernstein and Sheldon (2018)for the exponential-family experiment andBernstein and Sheldon (2019)for the linear-regression experiment. Both require specifying a working likelihood. The single release is the classical Gaussian mechanism, i.e., the sufficient statistics are perturbed with noise scaleσ=Δ​2​log⁡(1.25/δ)/ϵ\sigma=\Delta\sqrt{2\log(1.25/\delta)}\,/\,\epsilon(Dworket al.2014), whereΔ\Deltais theℓ2\ell_{2}sensitivity of the sufficient statistics over the bounded design.

## DP-Bootstrap(Wanget al.2025).

This is a private nonparametric bootstrap. It resamples the data, fits anℓ2\ell_{2}-regularizedMM-estimator with output perturbation on each bootstrap replicate, and releasesBBreplicates. The collection satisfiesμ\mu-GDP(Donget al.2022)asB→∞B\to\infty, with output-perturbation scaleσe=(2−2/e)​B​Δ/μ\sigma_{e}=\sqrt{(2-2/e)\,B}\,\Delta/\muwhereΔ\Deltais the sensitivity. To match the other methods, we turn the target(ϵ,δ)(\epsilon,\delta)into the largestμ\muwhose Gaussian-DP tradeoff function satisfiesδ​(ϵ;μ)≤δ\delta(\epsilon;\mu)\leq\delta(Donget al.2022, Corollary 2.13), so the release is calibrated to the same(ϵ,δ)(\epsilon,\delta)budget.

## S3.2Linear-regression and Exponential-family Experiments

## Data.

The regression study is that of Section5.1:Xi∼Uniform​[−1,1]3X_{i}\sim\mathrm{Uniform}[-1,1]^{3},Yi=Xi⊤​𝜷⋆+εiY_{i}=X_{i}^{\top}\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\star}+\varepsilon_{i}with𝜷⋆=(1.5,−1.5,1.0)\boldsymbol{\beta}^{\star}=(1.5,-1.5,1.0)andσε=0.5\sigma_{\varepsilon}=0.5, under Student-tt(df=3\mathrm{df}=3) and contaminated-normal (0.9​N​(0,σε2)+0.1​N​(0,64​σε2)0.9\,N(0,\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})+0.1\,N(0,64\sigma_{\varepsilon}^{2})) noise, withn∈{10​k,40​k}n\in\{10\text{k},40\text{k}\}. The exponential-family study is one-dimensional mean estimation under a correctly specified likelihood: a Gaussian modelXi∼N​(μ,σ2)X_{i}\sim N(\mu,\sigma^{2})with knownσ=0.5\sigma=0.5and estimandμ=1.5\mu=1.5, and an Exponential modelXi∼Exp​(λ)X_{i}\sim\mathrm{Exp}(\lambda)with estimand1/λ=2.01/\lambda=2.0, usingn∈{500,5000}n\in\{500,5000\}. Both studies useϵ∈{1,2,4}\epsilon\in\{1,2,4\}atδ=1/n\delta=1/nandR=100R=100replicates. In regression, the covariates lie in the public boxBx=1B_{x}=1(‖Xi‖≤Bx​p\|X_{i}\|\leq B_{x}\sqrt{p}). In the exponential-family study, each datum is truncated to a public interval[a,b][a,b]([−5,5][-5,5]for the Gaussian,[0,12][0,12]for the Exponential), which bounds the sufficient statistict​(x)=xt(x)=xat replace-one sensitivityΔ=b−a\Delta=b-aand, for the score-based methods (DP-SGLD and PGBB), the per-sample derivative is clipped atC=8C=8. For Bayesian methods, the credible interval is the95%95\%quantile interval of the draws.

In the regression study, DP-Bootstrap, DP-SGLD, and PGBB can use the robust Huber loss ofAvella-Medinaet al.(2023), whose score is bounded independently ofYYandβ\beta. In the exponential-family study, DP-Bootstrap, DP-SGLD, and PGBB clip the negative log-likelihood atCC. For the regression, they estimateβ\betaand the scaleσ\sigmawith Huber constantc=1.345c=1.345and consistency constantκ=0.71\kappa=0.71, and the per-sample gradient hasℓ2\ell_{2}norm bounded byCH=c2​p​Bx2+(12​(c2+κ))2C_{H}=\sqrt{c^{2}pB_{x}^{2}+(\tfrac{1}{2}(c^{2}+\kappa))^{2}}.

## PGBB.

The generatorGϕ:Dir​(γ​𝟏S)→ℝpG_{\phi}:\mathrm{Dir}(\gamma\mathbf{1}_{S})\to\mathbb{R}^{p}is a three-hidden-layer width-256256ReLU multilayer perceptron with the Dirichlet input re-concatenated after each hidden block(Shinet al.2021)and the linear output clipped to the parameter box. In the regression, it usesp=3p=3,S=20S=20blocks (S∈{5,20}S\in\{5,20\}in the block-count study of TableS6),T=10000T=10000iterations,K=1K=1Monte Carlo replicate, Poisson subsampling rateq=0.1q=0.1, the Adam optimizer with a cosine-decayed learning rate from5×10−45\times 10^{-4}to a1%1\%floor, gradient boundC=CHC=C_{H}, truncation ordersJ=32J=32andm=5m=5, andγ=γ⋆​(n,S)\gamma=\gamma^{\star}(n,S). In the exponential-family study it usesp=1p=1,S=20S=20,T=2000T=2000,K=1K=1,q=0.2q=0.2, gradient boundC=8C=8,J=30J=30,m=5m=5, andγ=1\gamma=1. The noise scaleσ\sigmais set by the bisection of SectionS1.5, and we releaseB=2000B=2000posterior draws.

## DP-SGLD.

DP-SGLD targets the Gibbs posterior rather than a parametric likelihood so that the comparison isolates the privacy mechanism. In the regression, it runsT=20000T=20000Langevin updates at temperingω=1\omega=1with step sizeη0\eta_{0}chosen soη0​n=20\eta_{0}n=20(η0=2×10−3\eta_{0}=2\times 10^{-3}atn=10n=10k and5×10−45\times 10^{-4}atn=40n=40k), Poisson subsampling rateq=0.1q=0.1matched to PGBB, and50%50\%burn-in. In the exponential-family study, it clips the per-sample score atC=8C=8, runsT=40000T=40000updates with50%50\%burn-in and thinning11.

## NoisyGibbs.

NoisyGibbs perturbs the sufficient statistics. In the regression, we release(𝐗⊤​𝐗,𝐗⊤​Y,Y⊤​Y)({\bf X}^{\top}{\bf X},\ {\bf X}^{\top}Y,\ Y^{\top}Y)together with the second and fourth moments of𝐗{\bf X}under the Gaussian mechanism, splitting the budget as(12,14,14)(\tfrac{1}{2},\tfrac{1}{4},\tfrac{1}{4})of(ϵ,δ)(\epsilon,\delta). For the sufficient-statistic block the replace-oneℓ2\ell_{2}sensitivity isΔ=2​p​(p+1)2​Bx4+p​Bx2​By2+By4\Delta=2\sqrt{\tfrac{p(p+1)}{2}B_{x}^{4}+pB_{x}^{2}B_{y}^{2}+B_{y}^{4}}, the factor22accounting for the simultaneous change of the removed and inserted record and the three terms being thep​(p+1)/2p(p+1)/2unique entries of𝐗⊤​𝐗{\bf X}^{\top}{\bf X}(each of sensitivityBx2B_{x}^{2}), theppentries of𝐗⊤​Y{\bf X}^{\top}Y(eachBx​ByB_{x}B_{y}), and the singleY⊤​YY^{\top}Yentry (of sensitivityBy2B_{y}^{2}). The moment vectorsm2,m4m_{2},m_{4}are sample means, so their sensitivities carry a1/n1/n:Δ2=2n​Bx2​p​(p+1)2\Delta_{2}=\tfrac{2}{n}B_{x}^{2}\sqrt{\tfrac{p(p+1)}{2}}andΔ4=2n​Bx4​D4\Delta_{4}=\tfrac{2}{n}B_{x}^{4}\sqrt{D_{4}}withD4=(p+34)D_{4}=\binom{p+3}{4}. Each release usesσ=Δ​2​log⁡(1.25/δ′)/ϵ′\sigma=\Delta\sqrt{2\log(1.25/\delta^{\prime})}/\epsilon^{\prime}for its split budget, and the noisym2,m4m_{2},m_{4}enterμt,Σt\mu_{t},\Sigma_{t}as the covariate momentsηi​j,ηi​j​k​l\eta_{ij},\eta_{ijkl}(equations (2)-(3) ofBernstein and Sheldon2019). The noise-aware Gibbs sampler runs400​k400\text{k}iterations with200​k200\text{k}burn-in and thinning1010, returning20​k20\text{k}draws. In the exponential-family study, the sufficient statistic is the scalart​(x)=xt(x)=x: we perturb its truncated sum by the Gaussian mechanism at replace-one sensitivityΔ=b−a\Delta=b-aand run the noise-aware Gibbs sampler ofBernstein and Sheldon (2018)for4​k4\text{k}iterations with half burn-in and thinning22.

## DP-Bootstrap.

For the regression study, we implement the DP-ERM-like implementation of DP-Bootstrap that reports the asymptotic interval that accounts for the known output-perturbation variance, i.e., Algorithm 2 ofWanget al.(2025). For the univariate exponential-family study, we implement Algorithm 3 ofWanget al.(2025). The target is the mean of the truncated variable on[a,b][a,b]wherea=−5,b=5a=-5,b=5in our study, so the base statistic is the bootstrap sample mean rather than anℓ2\ell_{2}-regularized ERM. Its replace-one sensitivity is thereforeΔ=(b−a)/n\Delta=(b-a)/n.

## S3.3Real Data Application

Both applications in Section6are differentially private quantile regressions over the levelsτ∈{0.1,0.3,0.5,0.7,0.9}\tau\in\{0.1,0.3,0.5,0.7,0.9\}, at privacy budgetsϵ∈{1,4}\epsilon\in\{1,4\}withδ=1/n\delta=1/nand 10 replicates. The U.S. Census is the returns-to-schooling regression for the years 1980 (n=65,023n=65,023) and 2000 (n=97,397n=97,397), where the design is𝐗=[1,schooling,experience,black]{\bf X}=[\,1,\text{schooling},\text{experience},\text{black}\,]withp=4p=4. Natality is the birthweight regression on the 2024 U.S. records (n≈1.94n\approx 1.94M; intercept plus1313covariates,p=14p=14). In the U.S. Census regression, the response is the log weekly wage,educis completed years of schooling,experis potential labor-market experience (age minus schooling minus six), andblackindicates Black respondents. Covariates are scaled into the unit box so thatBx=1B_{x}=1and‖Xi‖≤Bx​p\|X_{i}\|\leq B_{x}\sqrt{p}. This rescaling uses only each variable’s presumably publicly known range, i.e., the published Census and natality variable bounds. In both, PGBB usesS=5S=5and the variance-matched concentrationγ=γ⋆​(n,S)\gamma=\gamma^{\star}(n,S). We compare PGBB against DP-SGLD, where the non-private quantile fit serves as the reference. Both private methods use the convolution-smoothed (kernel) quantile lossρτ,h=ρτ∗Kh\rho_{\tau,h}=\rho_{\tau}*K_{h}(Tanet al.2022)at bandwidthh=0.1h=0.1, whose derivativeΦ​(r/h)−(1−τ)\Phi(r/h)-(1-\tau)(with the Gaussian kernelKK) lies in the open interval(τ−1,τ)(\tau-1,\tau)and is bounded in magnitude bymax⁡(τ,1−τ)\max(\tau,1-\tau), so a single record’s gradient is bounded bymax⁡(τ,1−τ)​Bx​p\max(\tau,1-\tau)\,B_{x}\sqrt{p}. The coefficients in TablesS8andS9and FiguresS1andS2are reported in interpretable units rather than the scaled design, witheducin %/yr,experin /yr, andblackin log-wage for the U.S. Census and grams for Natality. This conversion uses only the public variable ranges and so carries no privacy cost.

## PGBB (joint-τ\tau).

A singleτ\tau-joint generatorGϕ​(𝐮,τ)G_{\phi}({\bf u},\tau)is trained once to cover the whole quantile levels: it takes the Dirichlet weights and the levelτ\tauas input and outputsβ​(τ)\beta(\tau). Because one mechanism serves everyτ\tau, the per-record gradient has the uniform boundC=(maxτ⁡max⁡(τ,1−τ))​Bx​p=0.9​Bx​pC=(\max_{\tau}\max(\tau,1-\tau))B_{x}\sqrt{p}=0.9B_{x}\sqrt{p}, which dominatesmax⁡(τ,1−τ)\max(\tau,1-\tau)at every candidate level, so the entire process is released under one(ϵ,δ)(\epsilon,\delta)budget regardless of the number of levels. Training uses the Adam optimizer (learning rate10−310^{-3}),S=5S=5, Poisson subsampling rateq=0.01q=0.01,K=5K=5Monte Carlo draws, Beta-MGF truncationJ=32J=32, convolution-smoothing bandwidthh=0.1h=0.1, andT=5000T=5000(Census) /T=10000T=10000(Natality) iterations, withγ=γ⋆​(n,S)\gamma=\gamma^{\star}(n,S). After training, we drawB=2000B=2000posterior samples ofβ​(τ)\beta(\tau)at eachτ\tau.

## DP-SGLD (per-τ\tau).

DP-SGLD targets the tempered Gibbs posteriorp​(β∣{Yi,Xi})∝exp⁡{−ω​∑iρτ,h​(Yi−Xi⊤​β)}p(\beta\mid\{Y_{i},X_{i}\})\propto\exp\bigl\{-\omega\textstyle\sum_{i}\rho_{\tau,h}(Y_{i}-X_{i}^{\top}\beta)\bigr\}of the same convolution-smoothed loss, with scalerω\omega. We report the performance of DP-SGLD forω∈{1,0.1}\omega\in\{1,0.1\}. The tempering enters only the target posterior, whose precision scales asn​ωn\omega, and leaves the privacy accounting unchanged. Its per-sample score{Φ​(ri/h)−(1−τ)}​Xi\{\Phi(r_{i}/h)-(1-\tau)\}X_{i}is clipped at the per-level thresholdL​(τ)=max⁡(τ,1−τ)​Bx​pL(\tau)=\max(\tau,1-\tau)\,B_{x}\sqrt{p}. Because the smoothed score is strictly interior to[τ−1,τ][\tau-1,\tau], the clip never binds for boundedXiX_{i}. The score is perturbed by Gaussian noise at each ofT=200​kT=200\text{k}Langevin updates under the matched Poisson rateq=0.01q=0.01. The second half of the chain is retained after thinning by1010, giving10410^{4}iterates. Unlike PGBB, DP-SGLD is fit separately at each of theM=5M=5quantile levels. Rather than splitting the budget by basic(ϵ/M,δ/M)(\epsilon/M,\delta/M)composition, we account for the levels jointly at the Rényi differential privacy level(Mironov2017): theMMfits formM×TM\times TPoisson-subsampled Gaussian releases whose Rényi divergences add over a shared order grid(Birrellet al.2024), and a single Rényi-to-(ϵ,δ)(\epsilon,\delta)conversion(Asoodehet al.2021)certifies the entire released quantile process at the total budget(ϵ,1/n)(\epsilon,1/n).

## Non-private reference.

The comparison baseline is the ordinary (non-private) quantile fit on the same design and scaling. For the U.S. Census, whose samples are moderate, we use thestatsmodelspackage for quantile regression in Python. For Natality, wheren≈1.94n\approx 1.94M makes the hard check loss costly, we use the convolution-smoothed estimator(Tanet al.2022)solved by Newton iteration. Its95%95\%intervals come from a multiplier bootstrap that re-solves the smoothed fit underExp​(1)\mathrm{Exp}(1)weights (B=500B=500replicates, the2.5/97.52.5/97.5empirical percentiles). Both are evaluated at everyτ\tauand serve only as the non-private target.
